https://x.com/sleepinyourhat/status/1492912973035315206 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220213173117/https://twitter.com/sleepinyourhat/status/1492912973035315206)

## 2022-02-13 · reply to @sleepinyourhat

> The good news is that current NNs don't very closely resemble anything that we'd confidently recognize as phenomenally conscious, and they're many orders of magnitude less complex and less numerous.

https://x.com/sleepinyourhat/status/1492912967666524163 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220213173113/https://twitter.com/sleepinyourhat/status/1492912967666524163)

## 2022-02-13 · reply to @sleepinyourhat

> So, sure, it's plausible that a language model is 'slightly' conscious. But so is a thermostat. Or a compost pile. Figuring out *how much* is the hard part.

https://x.com/sleepinyourhat/status/1492912965548400650 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220213173337/https://twitter.com/sleepinyourhat/status/1492912965548400650)

## 2022-02-13 · reply to @sleepinyourhat

> I think that's fairly intuitive! Without that, it's really hard to see how we'd identify a sharp line delimiting what's phenomenally conscious and what's not.

https://x.com/sleepinyourhat/status/1492912964504068097 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220213173120/https://twitter.com/sleepinyourhat/status/1492912964504068097)

## 2022-02-13 · reply to @sleepinyourhat

> On that view, basically every system of any kind is very very 'slightly' phenomenally conscious. Even if it doesn't have any kind of self-awareness, it has 'experiences' to some (potentially extremely limited) degree.

https://x.com/sleepinyourhat/status/1492912963480662016 · [archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220213173110/https://twitter.com/sleepinyourhat/status/1492912963480662016)

> @tegan_maharaj That's obviously not the same at telling people to never apply for PhDs, and I get that that difference matters a great deal for DEI efforts at the minority of programs that *actually do* leave their students better off or move them into positions of real influence.

## 2022-02-10 · reply to @sleepinyourhat

> @tegan_maharaj And I'm assuming that the upside to starting a PhD program is much more widely understood than the risk. I'd guess that's at least as true if you're coming from a nontraditional background.

## 2022-02-09 · reply to @sleepinyourhat

> @tegan_maharaj And I definitely don't want to discourage everyone from PhDs in all cases, just warn that the average-case outcome is pretty bad in many fields and that you'll should probably do a *ton* of information-gathering about outcomes before you commit to one.

> @_florianmai Not that many! And they can be hard to find. If you're deeply committed to publishing research, you're probably stuck doing a PhD. But many (most) industries are filled with tons of jobs that are hard, have a real influence on the world, and aren't about publishing.

## 2022-01-25 · reply to @sleepinyourhat

> @emilymbender All I want to do at this point is push back against the view that working on this kind of 'AI safety'-related topic *necessarily* implies something strongly negative about your work, your motives, or your assumptions.

## 2022-01-25 · reply to @emilymbender

> @emilymbender A lot of more LM-centric AI safety projects—including this one, on my read—seem to be premised on the idea that we can't stop every misguided deployment of LMs, and so it's worth looking for ways to foresee and reduce the associated harms.

> @emilymbender @mark_riedl If you think language technologies could plausibly be deployed in much more intensive ways than they are now—whether or not that's what anyone involved wants—it's worth putting some energy into looking ahead at the new risks we expect to see emerge.

## 2022-01-10

> Periodic note: I don't have a specific postdoc job open now, but it's often possible to create one relatively quickly if there's a great opportunity. If you have a specific research goal that's *very closely* aligned with my group and you want to do a postdoc here, reach out!

## 2021-12-14 · reply to @sleepinyourhat

> @ethancaballero So, you can probably figure out some OOM estimates for compute at big universities, but getting fine-grained enough to compare across them with confidence will probably take &gt;&gt;100 emails per university, most of which won't get answered unless you have good bait.

## 2021-12-14 · reply to @sleepinyourhat

> @ethancaballero Universities have different mixes of centralized ML compute, compute owned by separate labs but managed/hosted centrally, and compute that's managed completely independently by separate labs.

> 🚨New Paper Alert🚨
